On the three `owner-missing` lines at 07:36:07Z: a seam in the e2e teardown, not a fixture's expected shape. `memberships.user_id` cascades from `public.users`, which cascades from `auth.users`, while `accounts` has no key to a user, so `deleteTenant`'s `deleteUser` removes the owner's membership and leaves the account and its `generation_jobs` rows standing; a job claimed after that moment meets an account with no owner row, `loadContext` answers "owner missing", and `failPermanently` logs it at error with the code. Item 8's fail-first update covers a job that is `queued` or `running` at that instant, so the three that got through sit in one of three windows: a claim between the update and `deleteUser` (the route claims through `claim_generation_job` on `status in ('queued','running') and next_run_at <= now()`, one tick's worth), a job that reached the table after the update (a webhook or `after()` hook landing late in the dev checkout flow), or a job the update did not match because its own write errored, which the helper does not check. The fixture account ids do not say which spec; the three in one second across two accounts read as one tick draining what two teardowns left. (3) Question 5, answered from the code, not from Stripe's defaults, because the code sets nothing about dunning: Checkout runs in subscription mode with `subscription_data: { metadata }` alone (`checkout-params.ts:62`), card only, no trial, no `pause_collection`, no `payment_behavior`, no `collection_method`, no `billing_cycle_anchor`; the webhook maps `invoice.payment_failed` to `past_due` and revokes only on `canceled`, `unpaid` or `incomplete_expired` (D-056, D-912, `entitlement.ts`). Under that shape a failed renewal leaves its invoice open on the subscription, Stripe retries that same invoice on the account's dunning schedule, and when a retry or the customer's card update pays it the subscription returns to `active` with its billing anchor unmoved: the period that invoice bills, the one that began on the renewal date, is the period paid for, so the paused time is covered by that payment and owed, and the next charge comes on the original date, not a new one. If the retries end, the dashboard's dunning setting decides whether Stripe marks the subscription `unpaid` (the invoice stays open and a later payment reactivates it on the same terms) or cancels it; either revokes our entitlement and a fresh subscription starts a new period. So D-2252's sentence is the first: "When the payment goes through, access resumes and the paused period is covered by it." The second behaviour would need `pause_collection` or voiding the open invoice and re-anchoring, which the code does not do and I would not add for launch. One UNVERIFIED on the record: the retry schedule and the end-of-retries action live in Haider's Stripe dashboard (Billing settings), not in the repo; the sentence holds under both end actions, and the runbook's 7a section could name the setting Haider chose.
